Continuing work from thread T-019c7342-a5df-7201-86ae-198e1c9aebb4. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/outline/extract.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/update/outline.rs @src/outline/mod.rs

Other relevant files: src/model/ui.rs, src/messages.rs, src/view/hit_test.rs, src/runtime/app.rs, src/syntax/parser.rs, src/model/document.rs

I have implemented the core of the Code Outline panel feature. This includes:
*   Created the `src/outline` module with data structures in `mod.rs` and extraction logic in `extract.rs`.
*   The extraction logic currently supports Rust, TypeScript/JavaScript, Python, Go, Java, PHP, C/C++, and Markdown. It uses tree-sitter AST walking and range-containment for nesting (or level-based nesting for Markdown).
*   Integrated extraction into the existing syntax worker thread in `src/runtime/app.rs`. The worker now returns `OutlineData` alongside `SyntaxHighlights` in the `ParseCompleted` message.
*   Added `OutlinePanelState` to `AppModel` (in `src/model/ui.rs` and `src/model/mod.rs`) to track collapse state, selection, and scroll offset.
*   Added an `outline` field to the `Document` struct in `src/model/document.rs`.
*   Implemented the `render_outline_panel` in `src/view/mod.rs`, which replaces the placeholder in the right dock. It uses a recursive `render_node` pattern similar to the file explorer.
*   Created `src/update/outline.rs` to handle `OutlineMsg` for jumping to symbols and toggling nodes.

I've identified several issues and improvements needed:
*   Clicking tree nodes currently does not collapse them; I need to investigate the hit-testing and message dispatch in `src/view/hit_test.rs` and `src/update/outline.rs`.
*   For Markdown, I want to strip the `#` prefixes from heading names in the outline for better ergonomics.
*   The outline panel does not properly support scrolling. I need to implement a reusable scrollable area abstraction, as there might be an existing pattern in the UI rendering code I should leverage.
*   I want to add YAML support to the outline extractor.
*   Double-clicking an item in the outline should scroll the active editor to that line.
*   I want to perform a thorough code review and scrutinize the current implementation before fixing these issues.

some minor issues with it: clicking tree nodes canot collapse them, for markdown, we wanna strip off the # prefixes (more ergonomic and is only for md and that will work anywhere most likely), the outline panel does not properly allow scorlling we should implement this properly so this scrollable area feature can be reused (we might have an abstraciton for this already, investigtate and ocnsult oracle taking into account the entire ui rendering code surface here), also would like yaml support here if possible, double clicking on something in the outline thing should perhapos scroll to that line in the active editor), lets first code review and scrutinize our implementation

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n12: pub enum OutlineKind {\n13:     Heading { level: u8 },\n14:     Module,\n15:     Class,\n16:     Struct,\n17:     Enum,\n18:     Interface,\n19:     Trait,\n20:     Function,\n21:     Method,\n22:     Property,\n23:     Field,\n24:     Constant,\n25:     EnumVariant,\n26:     Impl,\n27:     Namespace,\n28: }\n29: \n30: impl OutlineKind {\n31:     /// Short label for rendering in the outline tree\n32:     pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {\n33:         match self {\n34:             OutlineKind::Heading { level: 1 } => \"#\",\n35:             OutlineKind::Heading { level: 2 } => \"##\",\n36:             OutlineKind::Heading { level: 3 } => \"###\",\n37:             OutlineKind::Heading { level: 4 } => \"####\",\n38:             OutlineKind::Heading { level: 5 } => \"#####\",\n39:             OutlineKind::Heading { level: 6 } => \"######\",\n40:             OutlineKind::Heading { .. } => \"#\",\n41:             OutlineKind::Module => \"mod\",\n42:             OutlineKind::Class => \"class\",\n43:             OutlineKind::Struct => \"struct\",\n44:             OutlineKind::Enum => \"enum\",\n45:             OutlineKind::Interface => \"iface\",\n46:             OutlineKind::Trait => \"trait\",\n47:             OutlineKind::Function => \"fn\",\n48:             OutlineKind::Method => \"fn\",\n49:             OutlineKind::Property => \"prop\",\n50:             OutlineKind::Field => \"field\",\n51:             OutlineKind::Constant => \"const\",\n52:             OutlineKind::EnumVariant => \"var\",\n53:             OutlineKind::Impl => \"impl\",\n54:             OutlineKind::Namespace => \"ns\",\n55:         }\n56:     }\n57: }\n58: \n59: /// A range in the document (line/col are 0-based)\n60: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n61: pub struct OutlineRange {\n62:     pub start_line: usize,\n63:     pub start_col: usize,\n64:     pub end_line: usize,\n65:     pub end_col: usize,\n66: }\n67: \n68: /// A single node in the outline tree\n69: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n70: pub struct OutlineNode {\n71:     pub kind: OutlineKind,\n72:     pub name: String,\n73:     pub range: Outline panel update handlers\n2: \n3: use crate::commands::Cmd;\n4: use crate::messages::OutlineMsg;\n5: use crate::model::AppModel;\n6: use crate::outline::OutlineNode;\n7: \n8: /// Count total visible items in the outline tree (for navigation bounds)\n9: fn count_visible_items(\n10:     nodes: &[OutlineNode],\n11:     panel: &crate::model::OutlinePanelState,\n12: ) -> usize {\n13:     let mut count = 0;\n14:     for node in nodes {\n15:         count += 1;\n16:         if node.is_collapsible() && !panel.is_collapsed(node) {\n17:             count += count_visible_items(&node.children, panel);\n18:         }\n19:     }\n20:     count\n21: }\n22: \n23: /// Get the node at a given flattened visible index\n24: fn node_at_index<'a>(\n25:     nodes: &'a [OutlineNode],\n26:     panel: &crate::model::OutlinePanelState,\n27:     target: usize,\n28:     current: &mut usize,\n29: ) -> Option<&'a OutlineNode> {\n30:     for node in nodes {\n31:         if *current == target {\n32:             return Some(node);\n33:         }\n34:         *current += 1;\n35:         if node.is_collapsible() && !panel.is_collapsed(node) {\n36:             if let Some(found) = node_at_index(&node.children, panel, target, current) {\n37:                 return Some(found);\n38:             }\n39:         }\n40:     }\n41:     None\n42: }\n43: \n44: /// Handle outline panel messages\n45: pub fn update_outline(model: &mut AppModel, msg: OutlineMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {\n46:     match msg {\n47:         OutlineMsg::JumpToSymbol { line, col } => {\n48:             // Move cursor to the symbol and focus the editor\n49:             let editor = model.editor_mut();\n50:             editor.cursors[0].line = line;\n51:             editor.cursors[0].column = col;\n52:             editor.cursors[0].desired_column = None;\n53:             editor.clear_selection();\n54:             model.ensure_cursor_visible();\n55:             model.ui.focus_editor();\n56:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n57:         }\n58: \n59:         OutlineMsg::ToggleNode { line, name } => {\n60:             // Find node by line+name and toggle\n61:             let outline = model\n62:                 .editor_area\n63:                 .focused_document()\n64:                 .and_then(|doc| doc.outline.as_ref());\n65: \n66:             if let Some(outline) = outline {\n67:                 // Find the matching node\n68:                 fn find_node<'a>(\n69:                     nodes: &'a [OutlineNode],\n70:                     line: usize,\n71:                     name: &str,\n72:                 ) -> Option<&'a OutlineNode> {\n73:                     for node in nodes {\n74:                         if node.range.start_line == line && node.name == name {\n75:                             return Some(node);\n76:                         }\n77:                         if let Some(found) = find_node(&node.children, line, name) {\n78:                             return Some(found);\n79:                         }\n80:                     }\n81:                     None\n82:                 }\n83: \n84:                 if let Some(node) = find_node(&outline.roots, line, &name) {\n85:                     model.outline_panel.toggle_collapsed(node);\n86:                 }\n87:             }\n88:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n89:         }\n90: \n91:         OutlineMsg::SelectPrevious => {\n92:             if let Some(idx) = model.outline_panel.selected_index {\n93:                 if idx > 0 {\n94:                     model.outline_panel.selected_index = Some(idx - 1);\n95:                 }\n96:             } else {\n97:                 model.outline_panel.selected_index = Some(0);\n98:             }\n99:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n100:         }\n101: \n102:         OutlineMsg::SelectNext => {\n103:             let outline = model\n104:                 .editor_area\n105:                 .focused_document()\n106:                 .and_then(|doc| doc.outline.as_ref());\n107: \n108:             if let Some(outline) = outline {\n109:                 let total = count_visible_items(&outline.roots, &model.outline_panel);\n110:                 if let Some(idx) = model.outline_panel.selected_index {\n111:                     if idx + 1 < total {\n112:                         model.outline_panel.selected_index = Some(idx + 1);\n113:                     }\n114:                 } else {\n115:                     model.outline_panel.selected_index = Some(0);\n116:                 }\n117:             }\n118:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n119:         }\n120: \n121:         OutlineMsg::ExpandSelected => {\n122:             if let Some(idx) = model.outline_panel.selected_index {\n123:                 let outline = model\n124:                     .editor_area\n125:                     .focused_document()\n126:                     .and_then(|doc| doc.outline.as_ref());\n127: \n128:                 if let Some(outline) = outline {\n129:                     let mut current = 0;\n130:                     if let Some(node) =\n131:                         node_at_index(&outline.roots, &model.outline_panel, idx, &mut current)\n132:                     {\n133:                         if node.is_collapsible() {\n134:                             let key = crate::model::OutlinePanelState::node_key(node);\n135:                             model.outline_panel.collapsed.remove(&key);\n136:                         }\n137:                     }\n138:                 }\n139:             }\n140:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n141:         }\n142: \n143:         OutlineMsg::CollapseSelected => {\n144:             if let Some(idx) = model.outline_panel.selected_index {\n145:                 let outline = model\n146:                     .editor_area\n147:                     .focused_document()\n148:                     .and_then(|doc| doc.outline.as_ref());\n149: \n150:                 if let Some(outline) = outline {\n151:                     let mut current = 0;\n152:                     if let Some(node) =\n153:                         node_at_index(&outline.roots, &model.outline_panel, idx, &mut current)\n154:                     {\n155:                         if node.is_collapsible() {\n156:                             let key = crate::model::OutlinePanelState::node_key(node);\n157:                             model.outline_panel.collapsed.insert(key);\n158:                         }\n159:                     }\n160:                 }\n161:             }\n162:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n163:         }\n164: \n165:         OutlineMsg::OpenSelected => {\n166:             if let Some(idx) = model.outline_panel.selected_index {\n167:                 let outline = model\n168:                     .editor_area\n169:                     .focused_document()\n170:                     .and_then(|doc| doc.outline.as_ref());\n171: \n172:                 if let Some(outline) = outline {\n173:                     let mut current = 0;\n174:                     if let Some(node) =\n175:                         node_at_index(&outline.roots, &model.outline_panel, idx, &mut current)\n176:                     {\n177:                         let line = node.range.start_line;\n178:                         let col = node.range.start_col;\n179:                         // Jump to symbol and focus editor\n180:                         let editor = model.editor_mut();\n181:                         editor.cursors[0].line = line;\n182:                         editor.cursors[0].column = col;\n183:                         editor.cursors[0].desired_column = None;\n184:                         editor.clear_selection();\n185:                         model.ensure_cursor_visible();\n186:                         model.ui.focus_editor();\n187:                     }\n188:                 }\n189:             }\n190:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n191:         }\n192: \n193:         OutlineMsg::Scroll { lines } => {\n194:             let offset = model.outline_panel.scroll_offset;\n195:             if lines < 0 {\n196:                 model.outline_panel.scroll_offset = offset.saturating_sub((-lines) as usize);\n197:             } else {\n198:                 model.outline_panel.scroll_offset = offset + lines as usize;\n199:             }\n200:             Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n201:         }\n202: UI state - status bar, cursor blink, modals, and other UI concerns\n2: \n3: use super::editor_area::SplitDirection;\n4: use super::status_bar::{StatusBar, TransientMessage};\n5: use crate::editable::{EditConstraints, EditableState, StringBuffer};\n6: use crate::panel::DockPosition;\n7: use crate::theme::{list_available_themes, ThemeInfo};\n8: use std::path::PathBuf;\n9: use std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n10: \n11: // ============================================================================\n12: // Focus Management\n13: // ============================================================================\n14: \n15: /// Which top-level UI region currently has keyboard focus\n16: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]\n17: pub enum FocusTarget {\n18:     /// Main editor text area (default)\n19:     #[default]\n20:     Editor,\n21:     /// File tree sidebar (left dock's file explorer)\n22:     Sidebar,\n23:     /// A dock panel (right or bottom; left uses Sidebar for compatibility)\n24:     Dock(DockPosition),\n25:     /// Modal dialog (command palette, goto line, find/replace, etc.)\n26:     Modal,\n27: }\n28: \n29: /// Which UI region the mouse is currently hovering over\n30: ///\n31: /// Used for:\n32: /// - Determining scroll event targets (sidebar vs editor vs dock)\n33: /// - Setting appropriate cursor icons\n34: /// - Visual hover feedback\n35: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]\n36: pub enum HoverRegion {\n37:     /// Not hovering over any tracked region\n38:     #[default]\n39:     None,\n40:     /// Hovering over the sidebar file tree\n41:     Sidebar,\n42:     /// Hovering over the sidebar resize handle\n43:     SidebarResize,\n44:     /// Hovering over the editor text area\n45:     EditorText,\n46:     /// Hovering over the editor tab bar\n47:     EditorTabBar,\n48:     /// Hovering over the status bar\n49:     StatusBar,\n50:     /// Hovering over a modal dialog\n51:     Modal,\n52:     /// Hovering over a splitter (split view resize handle)\n53:     Splitter,\n54:     /// Hovering over a dock panel (right or bottom)\n55:     Dock(DockPosition),\n56:     /// Hovering over a dock resize handle\n57:     DockResize(DockPosition),\n58:     /// Hovering over a preview pane\n59:     Preview,\n60: }\n61: \n62: // ============================================================================\n63: // Modal System\n64: // ============================================================================\n65: \n66: /// Identifies which modal is currently active\n67: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n68: pub enum ModalId {\n69:     /// Command palette (Shift+Cmd+A)\n70:     CommandPalette,\n71:     /// Go to line dialog (Cmd+L)\n72:     GotoLine,\n73:     /// Find/Replace dialog (Cmd+F)\n74:     FindReplace,\n75:     /// Theme picker\n76:     ThemePicker,\n77:     /// File finder (Shift+Cmd+O) - fuzzy search files in workspace\n78:     FileFinder,\n79: }\n80: \n81: /// State for the command palette modal\n82: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n83: pub struct CommandPaletteState {\n84:     /// Editable state for the input field\n85:     pub editable: EditableState<StringBuffer>,\n86:     /// Index of selected command in filtered list\n87:     pub selected_index: usize,\n88: }\n89: \n90: impl Default for CommandPaletteState {\n91:     fn default() -> Self {\n92:         Self {\n93:             editable: EditableState::new(StringBuffer::new(), EditConstraints::single_line()),\n94:             selected_index: 0,\n95:         }\n96:     }\n97: }\n98: \n99: impl CommandPaletteState {\n100:     /// Get the input text (convenience accessor)\n101:     pub fn input(&self) -> String {\n102:         self.editable.text()\n103:     }\n104: \n105:     /// Set the input text (replaces content)\n106:     pub fn set_input(&mut self, text: &str) {\n107:         self.editable.set_content(text);\n108:     }\n109: }\n110: \n111: /// State for the goto line modal\n112: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n113: pub struct GotoLineState {\n114:     /// Editable state for the input field (numeric + colon only)\n115:     pub editable: EditableState<StringBuffer>,\n116: }\n117: \n118: impl Default for GotoLineState {\n119:     fn default() -> Self {\n120:         Self {\n121:             editable: EditableState::new(StringBuffer::new(), EditConstraints::goto_line()),\n122:         }\n123:     }\n124: }\n125: \n126: impl GotoLineState {\n127:     /// Get the input text (convenience accessor)\n128:     pub fn input(&self) -> String {\n129:         self.editable.text()\n130:     }\n131: \n132:     /// Set the input text (replaces content)\n133:     pub fn set_input(&mut self, text: &str) {\n134:         self.editable.set_content(text);\n135:     }\n136: }\n137: \n138: /// Which field is focused in find/replace\n139: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]\n140: pub enum FindReplaceField {\n141:     #[default]\n142:     Query,\n143:     Replace,\n144: }\n145: \n146: /// State for the find/replace modal\n147: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n148: pub struct FindReplaceState {\n149:     /// Editable state for the query field\n150:     pub query_editable: EditableState<StringBuffer>,\n151:     /// Editable state for the replacement field\n152:     pub replace_editable: EditableState<StringBuffer>,\n153:     /// Which field is currently focused\n154:     pub focused_field: FindReplaceField,\n155:     /// Whether replace mode is active (vs find-only)\n156:     pub replace_mode: bool,\n157:     /// Case-sensitive search\n158:     pub case_sensitive: bool,\n159: }\n160: \n161: impl Default for FindReplaceState {\n162:     fn default() -> Self {\n163:         Self {\n164:             query_editable: EditableState::new(StringBuffer::new(), EditConstraints::single_line()),\n165:             replace_editable: EditableState::new(\n166:                 StringBuffer::new(),\n167:                 EditConstraints::single_line(),\n168:             ),\n169:             focused_field: FindReplaceField::Query,\n170:             replace_mode: false,\n171:             case_sensitive: false,\n172:         }\n173:     }\n174: }\n175: \n176: impl FindReplaceState {\n177:     /// Get the query text (convenience accessor)\n178:     pub fn query(&self) -> String {\n179:         self.query_editable.text()\n180:     }\n181: \n182:     /// Set the query text (replaces content)\n183:     pub fn set_query(&mut self, text: &str) {\n184:         self.query_editable.set_content(text);\n185:     }\n186: \n187:     /// Get the replacement text (convenience accessor)\n188:     pub fn replacement(&self) -> String {\n189:         self.replace_editable.text()\n190:     }\n191: \n192:     /// Set the replacement text (replaces content)\n193:     pub fn set_replacement(&mut self, text: &str) {\n194:         self.replace_editable.set_content(text);\n195:     }\n196: \n197:     /// Get the currently focused editable state\n198:     pub fn focused_editable(&self) -> &EditableState<StringBuffer> {\n199:         match self.focused_field {\n200:             FindReplaceField::Query => &self.query_editable,\n201:             FindReplaceField::Replace => &self.replace_editable,\n202:         }\n203:     }\n204: \n205:     /// Get the currently focused editable state mutably\n206:     pub fn focused_editable_mut(&mut self) -> &mut EditableState<StringBuffer> {\n207:         match self.focused_field {\n208:             FindReplaceField::Query => &mut self.query_editable,\n209:             FindReplaceField::Replace => &mut self.replace_editable,\n210:         }\n211:     }\n212: \n213:     /// Toggle focus between query and replacement fields\n214:     pub fn toggle_field(&mut self) {\n215:         self.focused_field = match self.focused_field {\n216:             FindReplaceField::Query => FindReplaceField::Replace,\n217:             FindReplaceField::Replace => FindReplaceField::Query,\n218:         };\n219:     }\n220: }\n221: \n222: /// State for the theme picker modal\n223: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n224: pub struct ThemePickerState {\n225:     /// Index of selected theme in list\n226:     pub selected_index: usize,\n227:     /// Cached list of available themes (refreshed when modal opens)\n228:     pub themes: Vec<ThemeInfo>,\n229:     /// Original theme ID for restore on cancel\n230:     pub original_theme_id: String,\n231: }\n232: \n233: impl ThemePickerState {\n234:     /// Create a new theme picker state with the current theme for restore\n235:     pub fn new(current_theme_id: String) -> Self {\n236:         Self {\n237:             selected_index: 0,\n238:             themes: list_available_themes(),\n239:             original_theme_id: current_theme_id,\n240:         }\n241:     }\n242: }\n243: \n244: /// A file match result from fuzzy search\n245: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n246: pub struct FileMatch {\n247:     /// Full path to the file\n248:     pub path: PathBuf,\n249:     /// Just the file name (for display)\n250:     pub filename: String,\n[... omitted lines 251 to 386 ...]\n387:     }\n388: }\n389: \n390: // ============================================================================\n391: // Splitter Drag State\n392: // ============================================================================\n393: \n394: /// State for splitter (resize handle) dragging\n395: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n396: pub struct SplitterDragState {\n397:     /// Index of the splitter being dragged (into the splitters vec from compute_layout)\n398:     pub splitter_index: usize,\n399:     /// Local index within the container (which children boundary)\n400:     pub local_index: usize,\n401:     /// Starting mouse position when drag began (pixels)\n402:     pub start_position: (f32, f32),\n403:     /// Original ratios before drag started (for cancel/restore)\n404:     pub original_ratios: Vec<f32>,\n405:     /// Direction of the split (determines which axis to track)\n406:     pub direction: SplitDirection,\n407:     /// Container's total size in the drag direction (pixels)\n408:     pub container_size: f32,\n409:     /// Whether threshold exceeded (true = actively dragging with visual updates)\n410:     pub active: bool,\n411: }\n412: \n413: // ============================================================================\n414: // Sidebar Resize State\n415: // ============================================================================\n416: \n417: /// State for sidebar resize dragging\n418: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n419: pub struct SidebarResizeState {\n420:     /// Starting mouse X position when drag began\n421:     pub start_x: f64,\n422:     /// Original sidebar width (logical pixels) before drag started\n423:     pub original_width: f32,\n424: }\n425: \n426: /// UI state for the outline panel\n427: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n428: pub struct OutlinePanelState {\n429:     /// Index of selected item in flattened visible list\n430:     pub selected_index: Option<usize>,\n431:     /// Scroll offset (in items)\n432:     pub scroll_offset: usize,\n433:     /// Collapsed node keys: (kind_label, name, start_line)\n434:     pub collapsed: std::collections::HashSet<(&'static str, String, usize)>,\n435: }\n436: \n437: impl OutlinePanelState {\n438:     /// Get a stable key for a node (for tracking collapse state)\n439:     pub fn node_key(node: &crate::outline::OutlineNode) -> (&'static str, String, usize) {\n440:         (node.kind.label(), node.name.clone(), node.range.start_line)\n441:     }\n442: \n443:     /// Check if a node is collapsed\n444:     pub fn is_collapsed(&self, node: &crate::outline::OutlineNode) -> bool {\n445:         self.collapsed.contains(&Self::node_key(node))\n446:     }\n447: \n448:     /// Toggle collapse state of a node\n449:     pub fn toggle_collapsed(&mut self, node: &crate::outline::OutlineNode) {\n450:         let key = Self::node_key(node);\n451:         if !self.collapsed.remove(&key) {\n452:             self.collapsed.insert(key);\n453:         }\n454:     }\n455: }\n456: \n457: /// UI state - status messages and cursor animation\n458: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n459: pub struct UiState {\n460:     /// Message displayed in the status bar (legacy, kept for compatibility)\n461:     pub status_message: String,\n462:     /// Structured status bar with segments\n463:     pub status_bar: StatusBar,\n464:     /// Transient message with auto-expiry\n465:     pub transient_message: Option<TransientMessage>,\n466:     /// Whether the cursor is currently visible (for blinking)\n467:     pub cursor_visible: bool,\n468:     /// Timestamp of last cursor blink state change\n469:     pub last_cursor_blink: Instant,\n470:     /// Whether a file is currently being loaded\n471:     pub is_loading: bool,\n472:     /// Whether a file is currently being saved\n473:     pub is_saving: bool,\n474:     /// Currently active modal (if any)\n475:     pub active_modal: Option<ModalState>,\n476:     /// Last command palette state (persisted for quick re-execution)\n477:     pub last_command_palette: Option<CommandPaletteState>,\n478:     /// Last find/replace state (persisted for quick re-use)\n479:     pub last_find_replace: Option<FindReplaceState>,\n480:     /// File drag-and-drop state\n481:     pub drop_state: DropState,\n482:     /// Splitter (resize handle) drag state\n483:     pub splitter_drag: Option<SplitterDragState>,\n484:     /// Sidebar resize drag state\n485:     pub sidebar_resize: Option<SidebarResizeState>,\n486:     /// Which UI region has keyboard focus\n487:     pub focus: FocusTarget,\n488:     /// Which UI region the mouse is currently hovering over\n489:     pub hover: HoverRegion,\n490:     /// Lines that contained cursors in the previous frame (for damage tracking)\n491:     /// Used by cursor blink to determine which lines need redrawing\n492:     pub previous_cursor_lines: Vec<usize>,\n493: }\n494: \n495: impl UiState {\n496:     /// Create a new UI state with default settings\n497:     pub fn new() -> Self {\n498:         Self {\n499:             status_message: String::new(),\n500:             status_bar: StatusBar::new(),\n501:             transient_message: None,\n502:             cursor_visible: true,\n503:             last_cursor_blink: Instant::now(),\n504:             is_loading: false,\n505:             is_saving: false,\n506:             active_modal: None,\n507:             last_command_palette: None,\n508:             last_find_replace: None,\n509:             drop_state: DropState::default(),\n510:             splitter_drag: None,\n511:             sidebar_resize: None,\n512:             focus: FocusTarget::Editor,\n513:             hover: HoverRegion::None,\n514:             previous_cursor_lines: Vec::new(),\n515:         }\n516:     }\n517: \n518:     /// Create a UI state with an initial status message\n519:     pub fn with_status(message: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n520:         Self {\n521:             status_message: message.into(),\n522:             status_bar: StatusBar::new(),\n523:             transient_message: None,\n524:             cursor_visible: true,\n525:             last_cursor_blink: Instant::now(),\n526:             is_loading: false,\n527:             is_saving: false,\n528:             active_modal: None,\n529:             last_command_palette: None,\n530:             last_find_replace: None,\n531:             drop_state: DropState::default(),\n532:             splitter_drag: None,\n533:             sidebar_resize: None,\n534:             focus: FocusTarget::Editor,\n535:             hover: HoverRegion::None,\n536:             previous_cursor_lines: Vec::new(),\n537:         }\n538:     }\n539: \n540:     // =========================================================================\n541:     // Focus Management\n542:     // =========================================================================\n543: \n544:     /// Check if a modal is currently active\n545:     pub fn has_modal(&self) -> bool {\n546:         self.active_modal.is_some()\n547:     }\n548: \n549:     /// Open a modal (also sets focus to Modal)\n550:     pub fn open_modal(&mut self, state: ModalState) {\n551:         self.active_modal = Some(state);\n552:         self.focus = FocusTarget::Modal;\n553:     }\n554: \n555:     /// Close the active modal (returns focus to Editor)\n556:     pub fn close_modal(&mut self) {\n557:         self.active_modal = None;\n558:         self.focus = FocusTarget::Editor;\n559:     }\n560: \n561:     /// Set focus to the editor\n562:     pub fn focus_editor(&mut self) {\n563:         if self.focus != FocusTarget::Editor {\n564:             tracing::trace!(\"Focus changed: {:?} -> Editor\", self.focus);\n565:             self.focus = FocusTarget::Editor;\n566:         }\n567:     }\n568: \n569:     /// Set focus to the sidebar\n570:     pub fn focus_sidebar(&mut self) {\n571:         if self.focus != FocusTarget::Sidebar {\n572:             tracing::trace!(\"Focus changed: {:?} -> Sidebar\", self.focus);\n573:             self.focus = FocusTarget::Sidebar;\n574:         }\n575:     }\n576: \n577:     /// Set focus to a modal (prefer using open_modal instead)\n578:     pub fn focus_modal(&mut self) {\n579:         if self.focus != FocusTarget::Modal {\n580:             tracing::trace!(\"Focus changed: {:?} -> Modal\", self.focus);\n581:             self.focus = FocusTarget::Modal;\n582:         }\n583:     }\n584: \n585:     /// Set focus to a dock (right or bottom; left dock uses focus_sidebar)\n586:     pub fn focus_dock(&mut self, position: DockPosition) {\n587:         let target = FocusTarget::Dock(position);\n588:         if self.focus != target {\n589:             tracing::trace!(\"Focus changed: {:?} -> Dock({:?})\", self.focus, position);\n590:             self.focus = target;\n591:         }\n592:     }\n593: \n594:     /// Get the currently focused dock position, if any\n595:     pub fn focused_dock(&self) -> Option<DockPosition> {\n596:         match self.focus {\n597:             FocusTarget::Dock(pos) => Some(pos),\n598: true\n616:         } else {\n617:             false\n618:         }\n619:     }\n620: \n621:     /// Set the status message\n622:     pub fn set_status(&mut self, message: impl Into<String>) {\n623: The design follows a \"hit-test → dispatch\" pattern:\n7: //! 1. `hit_test_ui()` determines the highest-priority `HitTarget` at a point\n8: //! 2. Event handlers match on `(HitTarget, MouseButton, click_count)` to dispatch behavior\n9: //! 3. Handlers return `EventResult` to indicate consumption, focus changes, and redraw needs\n10: //!\n11: //! This replaces ad-hoc if/else chains in app.rs with explicit priority ordering\n12: //! and shared hit-testing logic across left/middle/right clicks.\n13: \n14: use std::path::PathBuf;\n15: \n16: use winit::event::{ElementState, MouseButton};\n17: use winit::keyboard::ModifiersState;\n18: \n19: use crate::commands::filter_commands;\n20: use crate::model::editor_area::{DocumentId, EditorId, fn new(x: f64, y: f64) -> Self {\n38:         Self { x, y }\n39:     }\n40: }\n41: \n42: /// A mouse event with all relevant context for hit-testing and dispatch\n43: #[derive(Clone, Debug)]\n44: pub **Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01RfbXnXeDdFVpZzNi67SPuG`

```
In `token-editor`, mouse clicks on the right dock's outline panel are currently **not dispatched** as `OutlineMsg`.

The search in `src/runtime/mouse.rs` shows that `HitTarget::DockContent` events only perform a generic `FocusDock` action (and return sidebar focus for the left dock). They do not yet differentiate between panel types or handle sub-item clicks (like nodes in an outline).

The `OutlineMsg::ToggleNode` and `OutlineMsg::JumpToSymbol` messages are defined and have handlers in `src/update/outline.rs`, but these are presently only triggered by keyboard shortcuts (e.g., Enter, Arrow keys) while the dock is focused, as seen in the feature documentation and lack of call sites in the mouse runtime.

Relevant files:
- [src/runtime/mouse.rs#L380-L392](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/mouse.rs#L380-L392) (Generic `DockContent` hit handling)
- [src/update/outline.rs#L45-L90](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/outline.rs#L45-L90) (Implementation of `JumpToSymbol` and `ToggleNode` handlers)
- [src/view/hit_test.rs#L205-L208](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/hit_test.rs#L205-L208) (Definition of `DockContent` hit target)
- [src/panel/dock.rs#L52-L60](file://~/code/token-editor/src/panel/dock.rs#L52-L60) (Panel ID definitions including `Outline`)
- [docs/feature/code-outline.md#L452-L456](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/feature/code-outline.md#L452-L456) (Keyboard-only mapping for outline actions)
```

## 1) TL;DR
The Outline panel is rendered correctly, but it’s currently “not interactive” because the input pipeline stops at `DockContent`/`HoverRegion::Dock(_)` and never performs outline-row hit-testing or dispatches `OutlineMsg`. Fix this by (a) adding an outline-specific hit target (or computing a clicked row inside the `DockContent` handler), (b) routing dock scroll to `OutlineMsg::Scroll` when the active dock panel is `PanelId::Outline`, and (c) clamping `scroll_offset` based on total visible items and viewport capacity.

Effort: **M (half day)** for click+scroll+clamp; **M (half day)** more if you also refactor collapse keys + per-doc state + YAML extractor.

---

## 2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### A. Click handling: add outline sub-item hit-testing + dispatch
Right now `HitTarget::DockContent { .. }` in `src/runtime/mouse.rs` only focuses the dock and returns. You need to turn a dock-content click into either:
- “toggle expand/collapse” (clicked on the indicator area), or
- “jump to symbol” (clicked on the row body),
and ideally also set `outline_panel.selected_index`.

**Simplest low-risk fix (minimal architecture disruption):**
1. Keep `HitTarget::DockContent` as-is.
2. In the `HitTarget::DockContent { position, active_panel_id }` arm, if `active_panel_id == PanelId::Outline`, compute which outline row was clicked from `event.pos` and dispatch appropriate `Msg::Outline(...)`.

Concretely:
- Mirror the layout math used by `render_outline_panel()` (`src/view/mod.rs`):
  - `row_height = model.metrics.file_tree_row_height`
  - `content_y = rect.y + row_height as f32 + 4.0`
  - `clicked_visual_row = ((event.pos.y - content_y) / row_height as f64).floor() as isize`
  - `clicked_index = model.outline_panel.scroll_offset + clicked_visual_row as usize`
- Determine whether the click was on the “toggle indicator”:
  - you need the node depth to compute `icon_x`. That’s the hard part if you only have a flat index.
  - easiest: don’t do per-depth chevron hit-testing initially; treat any click on a collapsible row as toggle if `x` is within a fixed small left gutter (e.g. first ~16px of the row content area). This is acceptable for MVP and matches your existing “+/-” rendering which is always near the left of the row.
  - better (still simple): add a helper that flattens visible nodes and returns `(node, depth, row_rect_x)` for the clicked index (details below).

**What to dispatch:**
- Add a new message that centralizes the “index → node → action” logic inside `update_outline()` (so mouse code doesn’t need to re-find nodes by `(line,name)`):
  - `OutlineMsg::Click { index: usize, click_count: u8, toggle: bool }`
  - Handler does:
    1) `model.outline_panel.selected_index = Some(index)`
    2) `let node = node_at_index(...)`
    3) if `toggle && node.is_collapsible()` => toggle collapse
    4) else if `click_count >= 2` (double click) or even on single click if you want => jump to node

This avoids duplicating tree search in mouse code and makes it easy to add double-click behavior.

**If you want proper chevron hit-testing (recommended):**
- Create a shared “flatten visible outline” helper (in `src/update/outline.rs` or a small `src/outline/flatten.rs`) that produces a vector of rows for the current document:
  ```rust
  struct OutlineRow<'a> { node: &'a OutlineNode, depth: usize }
  fn flatten_visible<'a>(roots: &'a [OutlineNode], panel: &OutlinePanelState, out: &mut Vec<OutlineRow<'a>>)
  ```
- Then `mouse.rs` can:
  1) compute `clicked_index`
  2) look up `rows[clicked_index]` to get `depth`
  3) compute `icon_x` exactly like rendering (depth * indent + rect.x + 8)
  4) check `event.pos.x` against `[icon_x, icon_x+14)` to decide toggle vs open

This matches the sidebar pattern (`clicked_on_chevron`) and fixes your “sub-item hit-testing” cleanly.

---

### B. Scroll handling: route dock wheel events to outline scroll
`WindowEvent::MouseWheel` in `src/runtime/app.rs` currently consumes `HoverRegion::Dock(_position)` and returns `None` (807–811 in your note). That guarantees outline scrolling will never happen.

**Fix:**
In the `HoverRegion::Dock(position)` match arm:
1) Get the active panel for that dock position:
   ```rust
   let dock = self.model.dock_layout.dock(position);
   let active = dock.active_panel();
   ```
2) If `active == Some(PanelId::Outline)` and `v_delta != 0`, dispatch:
   ```rust
   update(&mut self.model, Msg::Outline(OutlineMsg::Scroll { lines: v_delta }))
   ```
3) Otherwise keep consuming (return `None`) so dock scrolling doesn’t “bleed” into the editor.

This keeps your original intent (“consume dock scroll”) while making the outline panel actually scroll.

---

### C. Scroll bounds: clamp `scroll_offset` in `OutlineMsg::Scroll`
`OutlineMsg::Scroll` currently only saturating-subtracts on negative and freely increments on positive, so you can scroll past the end forever.

**Fix in `src/update/outline.rs`:**
After applying the delta, clamp to `[0, max_scroll]`, where:
- `total = count_visible_items(&outline.roots, &model.outline_panel)`
- `visible_capacity = number of visible rows in the outline viewport`
- `max_scroll = total.saturating_sub(visible_capacity)` (or `total.saturating_sub(visible_capacity).max(0)`)

**Computing `visible_capacity` (no new state required):**
Because `update_outline()` has access to `model.window_size`, `model.line_height`, `model.metrics.scale_factor`, and `dock_layout`, you can approximate the same height used by render:

For the **right dock** (Outline default):
- `status_bar_h = model.line_height`
- `bottom_dock_h = if bottom dock open { model.dock_layout.bottom.size(scale) } else { 0 }`
- `dock_content_h = window_h - status_bar_h - bottom_dock_h`
- subtract the outline title row (`row_height + 4`)
- `visible_capacity = max(1, dock_content_h / row_height)`

Then:
```rust
model.outline_panel.scroll_offset = model.outline_panel.scroll_offset.min(max_scroll);
```

Also consider clamping after *any* message that changes visibility count (toggle collapse/expand) so selection/scroll don’t become invalid.

---

### D. Double-click behavior: implement “single selects, double opens”
Your click tracking already supports double/triple click, but dock content clicks don’t use it.

Once you implement `OutlineMsg::Click { index, click_count, toggle }`:
- **Single click**: set selection (and optionally jump, your choice)
- **Double click**: jump to symbol (common UX)
- **Click on chevron / indicator area**: toggle regardless of click_count (like sidebar)

This matches your sidebar UX and uses the existing `ClickTracker` properly (`click_tracker.track_row_click(index)`).

---

### E. Reset stale state on document switch
`OutlinePanelState` is global, but outline content is per-document. There’s currently no reset logic; so `scroll_offset` / `selected_index` can point past the end when switching tabs.

Minimal, safe behavior:
- When the focused document changes, reset:
  - `model.outline_panel.scroll_offset = 0;`
  - `model.outline_panel.selected_index = None;`
Optionally also clear collapse state (see next section).

Where to hook:
- In the layout update that changes active editor/tab (`LayoutMsg::SwitchToTab`, focus group change), detect `old_doc_id != new_doc_id` and reset.
- Alternatively, do it in the doc-focus setter if you have one canonical place.

---

## 3) Rationale and trade-offs
- Keeping scroll routing based on `HoverRegion` is consistent with your existing architecture (sidebar vs editor vs dock). You don’t need to redesign input to be fully hit-test driven for wheel events yet.
- Centralizing outline click behavior in `update_outline()` (via `OutlineMsg::Click`) avoids duplicating fragile “find node by (line,name)” logic in mouse code and makes double-click support trivial.
- Clamping in the update handler guarantees correctness regardless of render timing or future render changes.

I’m intentionally not proposing a full “widget framework” or new dependencies; these are incremental changes aligned with your current patterns (sidebar hit-testing + scroll routing + Elm messages).

---

## 4) Risks and guardrails (important issues found)

### Collapse key (`HashSet<(&'static str, String, usize)>`) is safe but has real problems
- **Lifetime:** `'static` is safe here because `OutlineKind::label()` returns a literal; there’s no borrow bug.
- **But it’s unusual and has two practical issues:**
  1) **Performance:** `is_collapsed()` calls `node_key(node)` which **clones `node.name`** every time. Rendering calls `is_collapsed()` for many nodes every frame → unnecessary allocations.
  2) **Correctness collisions:** different kinds can share the same label (`Function` and `Method` both `"fn"`), so your collapse keys are not truly unique.

**Guardrail fix (recommended):**
- Change the key to be allocation-free and unambiguous, e.g.:
  - derive `Hash` for `OutlineKind` and `OutlineRange`, then:
    ```rust
    pub collapsed: HashSet<(OutlineKind, OutlineRange)>
    ```
  - `node_key = (node.kind, node.range)` (both Copy-ish once you derive properly)
This removes per-frame allocations and prevents label collisions.

### UTF-8 truncation bug in `render_outline_node`
You do:
```rust
let truncated = &node.name[..max_chars.saturating_sub(1)];
```
This slices by **bytes**, not chars, and can panic on non-ASCII names.

Guardrail: truncate by `chars()` or `unicode-segmentation` (but don’t add deps; `chars()` is fine for now).

---

## 5) Markdown heading names: verify + small hardening
In `extract.rs::parse_heading()`:
- The “primary path” (`inline` / `heading_content`) *should not* include `#` markers for ATX headings in tree-sitter-markdown, so it’s likely already correct.
- If users are seeing `#` in the displayed heading text, it may actually be coming from `OutlineKind::Heading { level }.label()` (`"#"`, `"##"`, …) which you intentionally render before the name.

If the problem is truly that the **name** contains `#` sometimes (e.g., unusual AST shapes, or headings like `## Title ##`), a safe hardening is to strip leading `#` in *both* paths:
```rust
text = t.trim().trim_start_matches('#').trim().to_string();
```
This won’t harm normal headings and makes behavior consistent.

---

## 6) YAML support: add a simple extractor (top-level keys as a tree)
`LanguageId::Yaml` exists but `extract_outline` falls through to empty.

MVP extractor design:
- Show YAML mapping keys as a tree
- Only handle mappings (ignore sequences or show sequence items as children if you want later)
- Use `OutlineKind::Property` (or `Field`) for YAML keys

Implementation sketch:
- Add `LanguageId::Yaml => extract_yaml_keys(root, source),`
- Walk AST looking for mapping pairs (tree-sitter-yaml commonly uses `block_mapping_pair` / `flow_pair` with fields `"key"` and `"value"`).
- For each pair:
  - `key_text = utf8_text(key_node).trim().trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'')`
  - Create `OutlineNode { kind: OutlineKind::Property, name: key_text.to_string(), range: node_range(&key_node or pair), children: ... }`
  - If the value is a mapping, recurse into it to populate children.

Keep it simple; expand to sequences later if needed.

---

## 7) Code quality: clean up `render_outline_node` (17 parameters)
This works, but it’s hard to maintain and easy to break.

Simple refactor (no architectural shift):
- Create a context struct similar to your `SidebarRenderContext`:
  ```rust
  struct OutlineRenderContext<'a> {
    rect: Rect,
    max_y: usize,
    row_height: usize,
    indent: f32,
    scroll_offset: usize,
    selected_index: Option<usize>,
    text_color: u32,
    selection_bg: u32,
    selection_fg: u32,
    folder_icon_color: u32,
    outline_panel: &'a OutlinePanelState,
  }
  ```
- Then `render_outline_node(frame, painter, node, ctx, y, visible_index, depth)`.

This makes future work (hover highlights, icons, inline filtering) much easier.

---

## 8) When to consider the advanced path (triggers)
Consider a more structured “panel widget” approach only if:
- you add more interactive dock panels (terminal, tasks) that need their own hit-testing/scrolling
- you need smooth pixel scrolling (not item-based) or inertial scrolling
- you want per-document persisted outline UI state (collapsed/scroll/selection) across switches

---

## 9) Optional advanced path (only if you want it)
Unify dock interactions into the hit-test system:
- Add dock hit-testing to `hit_test_ui()` (right now it appears to only test modal/status/sidebar/splitters/previews/editor groups).
- Introduce `HitTarget::OutlineRow { index, depth, on_chevron }`.
- Route clicks purely by `HitTarget`, matching the sidebar architecture exactly.

This produces the cleanest code long-term, but the “simple path” above is enough to fix your immediate bugs without a bigger rewrite.

### Code Review: Outline Panel Implementation

#### 🔴 Critical Bugs

**1. Clicking outline nodes does nothing useful** — `DockContent` click handler (`mouse.rs:380-392`) only calls `FocusDock`, never dispatches `OutlineMsg::ToggleNode` or `JumpToSymbol`. No sub-item hit-testing exists for outline rows.

**2. Scrolling is completely broken** — `MouseWheel` for `HoverRegion::Dock(_)` (`app.rs:807-811`) consumes the event with `None` — never routes to `OutlineMsg::Scroll`.

**3. Scroll offset has no upper bound** — `OutlineMsg::Scroll` handler (`outline.rs:193-201`) freely increments `scroll_offset` with no max clamp; you can scroll past the end.

**4. UTF-8 panic in truncation** — `render_outline_node` does `&node.name[..max_chars.saturating_sub(1)]` which slices by **bytes**, not chars. Will panic on non-ASCII symbol names (e.g. `résumé`, CJK identifiers).

#### 🟡 Functional Gaps

**5. No double-click → jump-to-symbol** — `DockContent` doesn't differentiate click count; even once click handling is added, double-click needs explicit routing.

**6. Stale state on document switch** — `OutlinePanelState` is global. When switching tabs, `scroll_offset` and `selected_index` are never reset, pointing at stale positions.

**7. Collapse key collision** — `OutlinePanelState` keys use `(label, name, line)` but `Function` and `Method` both have label `"fn"`, so a function and method on the same line with the same name would share collapse state. Should use `OutlineKind` directly (derive `Hash`).

**8. Per-frame allocations in `is_collapsed`** — `node_key()` clones `node.name` (allocating a `String`) on every call. This runs for every visible node every frame.

**9. No YAML outline** — `LanguageId::Yaml` falls through to `Vec::new()` despite tree-sitter-yaml being available.

#### 🟢 Cosmetic / Quality

**10. Markdown `#` in names** — The `parse_heading` primary path correctly extracts `inline`/`heading_content` which shouldn't include `#` markers. The fallback path also strips `#`. Should be fine, but the `OutlineKind::Heading` label renders `#`/`##` prefixes before the name — so the user sees `# Title` which is redundant. Consider using a heading icon like `H1`/`H2` or just the name.

**11. `render_outline_node` has 17 parameters** — Hard to maintain. A `SidebarRenderContext`-like struct already exists for the sidebar and should be replicated here.

#### Summary of Fixes Needed

| Issue | Severity | Fix |
|-------|----------|-----|
| Click handling | 🔴 | Add row hit-testing in `DockContent` handler, dispatch `OutlineMsg` |
| Scroll routing | 🔴 | Route `HoverRegion::Dock` scroll to `OutlineMsg::Scroll` when active panel is Outline |
| Scroll clamp | 🔴 | Clamp `scroll_offset` to `max(0, total_items - visible_capacity)` |
| UTF-8 truncation | 🔴 | Use `.chars().take(n)` instead of byte slicing |
| Double-click | 🟡 | Pass click count, jump on double-click |
| Stale state reset | 🟡 | Reset `scroll_offset`/`selected_index` on tab/doc switch |
| Collapse key | 🟡 | Derive `Hash` on `OutlineKind`, use `(OutlineKind, OutlineRange)` |
| Per-frame allocs | 🟡 | Follows from collapse key fix |
| YAML support | 🟡 | Add YAML key extractor |
| Heading labels | 🟢 | Change heading label from `#` to `H1`/`H2`/etc. |
| 17-param function | 🟢 | Extract `OutlineRenderContext` struct |

Want me to start fixing these issues? I'd suggest tackling them in priority order: click handling → scroll routing/clamp → UTF-8 fix → double-click → state reset → collapse key → render context → YAML → heading labels.

```
The `DockLayout` struct and its associated logic for computing dock content rectangles are primarily split between the model definition and the view/rendering logic.

### DockLayout and Sizing
The `DockLayout` struct is defined in `src/panel/dock.rs`. It manages three `Dock` instances (left, right, bottom). Each `Dock` stores its `is_open` state and `size_logical`. The physical size is computed via `Dock::size(scale_factor)`.

- [src/panel/dock.rs#L211-L220](file://~/code/token-editor/src/panel/dock.rs#L211-L220) - `DockLayout` struct definition.
- [src/panel/dock.rs#L94-L110](file://~/code/token-editor/src/panel/dock.rs#L94-L110) - `Dock` struct definition.
- [src/panel/dock.rs#L163-L169](file://~/code/token-editor/src/panel/dock.rs#L163-L169) - `Dock::size` calculation (returns 0 if closed).

### Rect Computation for Rendering
While `DockRects` in `src/view/geometry.rs` provides a centralized `compute` method, the actual rendering loop in `src/view/mod.rs` currently re-calculates the `Rect` on the fly when calling `render_dock`.

For the **right dock**, the content rectangle is determined by subtracting the dock's width from the window width and subtracting the status bar and bottom dock heights.

- [src/view/mod.rs#L2828-L2833](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L2828-L2833) - Right dock `Rect` calculation for rendering.
- [src/view/mod.rs#L1152-L1155](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L1152-L1155) - Dispatching the `rect` to `render_outline_panel`.
- [src/view/geometry.rs#L1232-L1294](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L1232-L1294) - Centralized `DockRects::compute` logic (mirrors the rendering logic).

### Viewport and Hit-Testing
The `AppModel` uses these widths to shrink the editor's visible area, and the runtime uses similar logic for mouse hit-testing (distinguishing between content and resize handles).

- [src/model/mod.rs#L568-L604](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/mod.rs#L568-L604) - `AppModel::resize` adjusting the editor viewport for docks.
- [src/runtime/app.rs#L350-L400](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L350-L400) - Hit-testing logic for right/bottom docks.
```

    {
      "text": "<loaded_skill name=\"dispatching-parallel-agents\">\n# dispatching-parallel-agents Skill\n\n\n# Dispatching Parallel Agents\n\n## Overview\n\nWhen you have multiple unrelated failures (different test files, different subsystems, different bugs), investigating them sequentially wastes time. Each investigation is independent and can happen in parallel.\n\n**Core principle:** Dispatch one agent per independent problem domain. Let them work concurrently.\n\n## When to Use\n\n```dot\ndigraph when_to_use {\n    \"Multiple failures?\" [shape=diamond];\n    \"Are they independent?\" [shape=diamond];\n    \"Single agent investigates all\" [shape=box];\n    \"One agent per problem domain\" [shape=box];\n    \"Can they work in parallel?\" [shape=diamond];\n    \"Sequential agents\" [shape=box];\n    \"Parallel dispatch\" [shape=box];\n\n    \"Multiple failures?\" -> \"Are they independent?\" [label=\"yes\"];\n    \"Are they independent?\" -> \"Single agent investigates all\" [label=\"no - related\"];\n    \"Are they independent?\" -> \"Can they work in parallel?\" [label=\"yes\"];\n    \"Can they work in parallel?\" -> \"Parallel dispatch\" [label=\"yes\"];\n    \"Can they work in parallel?\" -> \"Sequential agents\" [label=\"no - shared state\"];\n}\n```\n\n**Use when:**\n- 3+ test files failing with different root causes\n- Multiple subsystems broken independently\n- Each problem can be understood without context from others\n- No shared state between investigations\n\n**Don't use when:**\n- Failures are related (fix one might fix others)\n- Need to understand full system state\n- Agents would interfere with each other\n\n## The Pattern\n\n### 1. Identify Independent Domains\n\nGroup failures by what's broken:\n- File A tests: Tool approval flow\n- File B tests: Batch completion behavior\n- File C tests: Abort functionality\n\nEach domain is independent - fixing tool approval doesn't affect abort tests.\n\n### 2. Create Focused Agent Tasks\n\nEach agent gets:\n- **Specific scope:** One test file or subsystem\n- **Clear goal:** Make these tests pass\n- **Constraints:** Don't change other code\n- **Expected output:** Summary of what you found and fixed\n\n### 3. Dispatch in Parallel\n\n```typescript\n// In Claude Code / AI environment\nTask(\"Fix agent-tool-abort.test.ts failures\")\nTask(\"Fix batch-completion-behavior.test.ts failures\")\nTask(\"Fix tool-approval-race-conditions.test.ts failures\")\n// All three run concurrently\n```\n\n### 4. Review and Integrate\n\nWhen agents return:\n- Read each summary\n- Verify fixes don't conflict\n- Run full test suite\n- Integrate all changes\n\n## Agent Prompt Structure\n\nGood agent prompts are:\n1. **Focused** - One clear problem domain\n2. **Self-contained** - All context needed to understand the problem\n3. **Specific about output** - What should the agent return?\n\n```markdown\nFix the 3 failing tests in src/agents/agent-tool-abort.test.ts:\n\n1. \"should abort tool with partial output capture\" - expects 'interrupted at' in message\n2. \"should handle mixed completed and aborted tools\" - fast tool aborted instead of completed\n3. \"should properly track pendingToolCount\" - expects 3 results but gets 0\n\nThese are timing/race condition issues. Your task:\n\n1. Read the test file and understand what each test verifies\n2. Identify root cause - timing issues or actual bugs?\n3. Fix by:\n   - Replacing arbitrary timeouts with event-based waiting\n   - Fixing bugs in abort implementation if found\n   - Adjusting test expectations if testing changed behavior\n\nDo NOT just increase timeouts - find the real issue.\n\nReturn: Summary of what you found and what you fixed.\n```\n\n## Common Mistakes\n\n**❌ Too broad:** \"Fix all the tests\" - agent gets lost\n**✅ Specific:** \"Fix agent-tool-abort.test.ts\" - focused scope\n\n**❌ No context:** \"Fix the race condition\" - agent doesn't know where\n**✅ Context:** Paste the error messages and test names\n\n**❌ No constraints:** Agent might refactor everything\n**✅ Constraints:** \"Do NOT change production code\" or \"Fix tests only\"\n\n**❌ Vague output:** \"Fix it\" - you don't know what changed\n**✅ Specific:** \"Return summary of root cause and changes\"\n\n## When NOT to Use\n\n**Related failures:** Fixing one might fix others - investigate together first\n**Need full context:** Understanding requires seeing entire system\n**Exploratory debugging:** You don't know what's broken yet\n**Shared state:** Agents would interfere (editing same files, using same resources)\n\n## Real Example from Session\n\n**Scenario:** 6 test failures across 3 files after major refactoring\n\n**Failures:**\n- agent-tool-abort.test.ts: 3 failures (timing issues)\n- batch-completion-behavior.test.ts: 2 failures (tools not executing)\n- tool-approval-race-conditions.test.ts: 1 failure (execution count = 0)\n\n**Decision:** Independent domains - abort logic separate from batch completion separate from race conditions\n\n**Dispatch:**\n```\nAgent 1 → Fix agent-tool-abort.test.ts\nAgent 2 → Fix batch-completion-behavior.test.ts\nAgent 3 → Fix tool-approval-race-conditions.test.ts\n```\n\n**Results:**\n- Agent 1: Replaced timeouts with event-based waiting\n- Agent 2: Fixed event structure bug (threadId in wrong place)\n- Agent 3: Added wait for async tool execution to complete\n\n**Integration:** All fixes independent, no conflicts, full suite green\n\n**Time saved:** 3 problems solved in parallel vs sequentially\n\n## Key Benefits\n\n1. **Parallelization** - Multiple investigations happen simultaneously\n2. **Focus** - Each agent has narrow scope, less context to track\n3. **Independence** - Agents don't interfere with each other\n4. **Speed** - 3 problems solved in time of 1\n\n## Verification\n\nAfter agents return:\n1. **Review each summary** - Understand what changed\n2. **Check for conflicts** - Did agents edit same code?\n3. **Run full suite** - Verify all fixes work together\n4. **Spot check** - Agents can make systematic errors\n\n## Real-World Impact\n\nFrom debugging session (2025-10-03):\n- 6 failures across 3 files\n- 3 agents dispatched in parallel\n- All investigations completed concurrently\n- All fixes integrated successfully\n- Zero conflicts between agent changes\n\n\nBase directory for this skill: file://~/.claude/plugins/cache/temp_git_1770922172287_5b9o3v/skills/dispatching-parallel-agents\nRelative paths in this skill (e.g., scripts/, reference/) are relative to this base directory.\n\n<skill_files>\n<file>~/.claude/plugins/cache/temp_git_1770922172287_5b9o3v/skills/dispatching-parallel-agents/.orphaned_at</file>\n</skill_files>\n</loaded_skill>",

Add YAML extraction functions at the end of the file (before `#[cfg(test)]`):\n\n```rust\n// =============================================================================\n// YAML symbol extraction\n// =============================================================================\n\nfn extract_yaml_symbols(root: Node, source: &str) -> Vec<FlatSymbol> {\n    let mut symbols = Vec::new();\n    collect_yaml_symbols(root, source, &mut symbols);\n    symbols\n}\n\nfn collect_yaml_symbols(node: Node, source: &str, symbols: &mut Vec<FlatSymbol>) {\n    match node.kind() {\n        \"block_mapping_pair\" | \"flow_pair\" => {\n            if let Some(key_node) = child_by_field(&node, \"key\") {\n                if let Some(key_text) = node_name(&key_node, source) {\n                    let name = key_text\n                        .trim()\n                        .trim_matches('\"')\n                        .trim_matches('\\'');\n                    if !name.is_empty() {\n                        symbols.push(flat_sym(OutlineKind::Property, name, &node));\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        _ => {}\n    }\n\n    let mut cursor = node.walk();\n    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {\n        collect_yaml_symbols(child, source, symbols);\n    }\n}\n```\n\nThe `build_tree_by_containment` function will automatically nest child keys under parent keys based on byte ranges.\n\nReturn a summary of changes made.",

All clean — no warnings, no errors. Here's a summary of everything fixed:

### Changes Made

| File | Fix |
|------|-----|
| **`src/outline/mod.rs`** | Added `Hash` to `OutlineKind` + `OutlineRange`; changed heading labels from `#`/`##` → `H1`/`H2` |
| **`src/outline/extract.rs`** | Added YAML outline extractor (`block_mapping_pair`/`flow_pair` → nested `Property` tree) |
| **`src/model/ui.rs`** | Changed collapse key from `(&'static str, String, usize)` to `(OutlineKind, OutlineRange)` — fixes label collisions + per-frame allocations |
| **`src/messages.rs`** | Added `OutlineMsg::ClickRow { index, click_count, on_chevron }` |
| **`src/update/outline.rs`** | Implemented `ClickRow` handler (select + chevron toggle + double-click jump); added scroll bounds clamping; clamped after expand/collapse |
| **`src/update/layout.rs`** | Reset `scroll_offset`/`selected_index` on tab switch and group focus |
| **`src/runtime/mouse.rs`** | `DockContent` click now does row hit-testing for outline, dispatches `ClickRow` with chevron detection |
| **`src/runtime/app.rs`** | Dock scroll events now route to `OutlineMsg::Scroll` when outline is active |
| **`src/view/mod.rs`** | Fixed UTF-8 truncation panic (byte→char slicing); extracted `OutlineRenderContext` struct (17 params → 7) |

## Assistant

There it is — **`hit_test_ui` has no dock hit-testing at all!** It checks modal → status bar → sidebar → splitters → previews → editor groups. The right dock area is never tested, so clicks there fall through to `hit_test_groups`, which returns `EditorContent` for any point inside a group rect — and the dock area overlaps the editor area in the coordinate space.

## Assistant

Confirmed — the only lint error is a pre-existing issue in `recent_files.rs`, not from our changes. Our code passes cleanly.

**Root cause found and fixed**: `hit_test_ui()` had no dock hit-testing at all. Clicks on the right dock area fell through to `hit_test_groups()`, which matched them as `EditorContent` since editor group rects extend under the dock in coordinate space.

**Fix**: Added `hit_test_docks()` function that checks right and bottom dock rectangles, and inserted it into `hit_test_ui()` at priority 5 — after sidebar but before splitters/editor groups. Dock clicks now correctly produce `HitTarget::DockContent` with the active panel ID, which the mouse handler then routes to `OutlineMsg::ClickRow`.

{
  "path": "~/code/token-editor/docs/feature/tree-navigation-abstraction.md",
  "content": "# Tree Navigation Abstraction\n\nUnified keyboard/mouse navigation for tree-style panels: outline, file explorer, todo list, and future tree views.\n\n> **Status:** Planned\n> **Priority:** P2\n> **Effort:** M (3–5 days)\n> **Created:** 2026-02-19\n> **Updated:** 2026-02-19\n> **Milestone:** 3 - Workspace Features\n\n---\n\n## Overview\n\n### Current State\n\nThe editor has multiple tree-style panels that each implement navigation independently:\n\n- **File explorer** (left dock): Full arrow key navigation (Up/Down to move selection, Left/Right to collapse/expand, Enter to open). Implemented in `src/update/workspace.rs` with `WorkspaceMsg::SelectPrevious`, `SelectNext`, `OpenOrToggle`, etc.\n- **Outline panel** (right dock): Mouse click and double-click working. Keyboard navigation messages exist (`OutlineMsg::SelectPrevious/Next`, `ExpandSelected`, `CollapseSelected`, `OpenSelected`) but **no keybinding dispatch** connects arrow keys to these messages when the outline panel is focused.\n- **Future panels** (todo list, bookmarks, search results): Will need the same tree navigation pattern.\n\n### Problem\n\nEach panel re-implements the same navigation logic:\n- `count_visible_items()` / `node_at_index()` tree flattening\n- Scroll-into-view when selection moves past viewport edges\n- Expand/collapse toggle on Left/Right arrows\n- Enter to activate/open\n- Escape to return focus to editor\n\nThis creates duplication and inconsistency. The outline panel currently has no keyboard navigation because the key dispatch in `src/runtime/input.rs` doesn't route arrow keys to `OutlineMsg` when `FocusTarget::Dock(Right)` is active.\n\n### Goals\n\n1. **Immediate**: Wire arrow key dispatch to existing `OutlineMsg` handlers when outline panel is focused\n2. **Short-term**: Extract a shared `TreeNavState` and `TreeNavMsg` abstraction\n3. **Long-term**: New tree panels get navigation \"for free\" by implementing a `TreeDataSource` trait\n\n### Non-Goals\n\n- Drag-and-drop reordering within trees\n- Multi-select in trees (future consideration)\n- Virtualized rendering (current approach of skipping off-screen items is sufficient)\n\n---\n\n## Architecture\n\n### Proposed Trait\n\n```rust\n/// Data source for a navigable tree view\ntrait TreeDataSource {\n    type NodeId: Clone + Eq + Hash;\n\n    fn root_count(&self) -> usize;\n    fn root_ids(&self) -> Vec<Self::NodeId>;\n    fn children(&self, id: &Self::NodeId) -> &[Self::NodeId];\n    fn is_expandable(&self, id: &Self::NodeId) -> bool;\n    fn node_label(&self, id: &Self::NodeId) -> &str;\n}\n```\n\n### Shared Navigation State\n\n```rust\n/// Reusable state for any tree-navigable panel\nstruct TreeNavState {\n    selected_index: Option<usize>,\n    scroll_offset: usize,\n    collapsed: HashSet<NodeKey>,\n}\n```\n\n### Key Dispatch\n\nThe immediate fix (Phase 1) wires `FocusTarget::Dock(Right)` arrow keys to outline messages in `src/runtime/input.rs`:\n\n| Key | Outline Action |\n|-----|---------------|\n| `↑` | `OutlineMsg::SelectPrevious` |\n| `↓` | `OutlineMsg::SelectNext` |\n| `→` | `OutlineMsg::ExpandSelected` |\n| `←` | `OutlineMsg::CollapseSelected` |\n| `Enter` | `OutlineMsg::OpenSelected` |\n| `Escape` | Focus editor |\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Plan\n\n### Phase 1: Wire Outline Arrow Keys (0.5 day)\n\nConnect existing `OutlineMsg` handlers to keyboard input when the outline panel has focus.\n\n**Files:**\n- `src/runtime/input.rs` — Add key dispatch for `FocusTarget::Dock(Right)` when active panel is `PanelId::Outline`\n\n### Phase 2: Extract TreeNavState (1–2 days)\n\nFactor the shared navigation pattern out of `OutlinePanelState` and sidebar's `Workspace`:\n\n- `src/tree_nav.rs` — `TreeNavState`, `TreeNavMsg` enum, `update_tree_nav()` pure function\n- Update `OutlinePanelState` to wrap `TreeNavState`\n- Update `Workspace` selection/scroll to use `TreeNavState`\n\n### Phase 3: TreeDataSource Trait (1–2 days)\n\nDefine the trait and implement it for:\n- `OutlineData` (outline nodes)\n- `FileTree` (file explorer nodes)\n- Future: `TodoList`, `SearchResults`, `Bookmarks`\n\n### Phase 4: Unified Tree Renderer (optional, future)\n\nExtract `render_tree_panel()` that takes a `TreeDataSource` + `TreeNavState` and handles:\n- Indented rows with expand/collapse indicators\n- Selection highlighting\n- Scroll offset\n- Hit-testing for clicks\n\n---\n\n## Dependencies\n\n- Outline panel (✅ implemented)\n- Dock focus system (✅ implemented)\n- File explorer sidebar (✅ existing, to be refactored)\n\n## References\n\n- Current outline update: `src/update/outline.rs`\n- Current sidebar update: `src/update/workspace.rs`\n- Sidebar rendering context: `SidebarRenderContext` in `src/view/mod.rs`\n- Outline rendering context: `OutlineRenderContext` in `src/view/mod.rs`\n- Panel UI abstraction doc: `docs/feature/panel-ui-abstraction.md`\n"
}
```
